# M13 — independent review package

**Purpose.** One bounded, independent review of a finished study. The question is whether its implementation,
analysis and conclusions are scientifically defensible. Nothing is being designed or rerun.

**State.**
- M13 was frozen (`8619d71`), run once, and recorded (`b9a05e0`).
- This package adds files only, in `experiments/m13_generalization/review/`. No frozen file, result, agent or
  application file was changed to make it.
- The single-file version, [M13_REVIEW_BUNDLE.md](M13_REVIEW_BUNDLE.md), holds this cover followed by every file
  verbatim, with its SHA-256. It is generated by [build_bundle.mjs](build_bundle.mjs).

## How to read this package

Everything in this cover, in RESULTS.md and in PROJECT_STATE.md was written by the study's designer. Treat it as a
claim to check against the specification, the source and the raw result.

The designer is Claude, working for the project owner. The same designer wrote the driver, the independent check and
the descriptive analyses.

Earlier outside reviews of this project restated the cover and missed defects that were visible in the code. Please
work from the appendices, or from a clone of the repository at the package commit.

**Please classify each finding:**

| Class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **MATERIAL** | It would change a verdict line, the summary statement, or a claim the record makes |
| **MINOR** | Wording, presentation or a missing caveat that changes no claim |
| **NOTE** | An observation with no required change |

Only MATERIAL correctness issues will be addressed after the review, by dated amendment, with the original text kept.

## The study in one paragraph

A small testbed: 24-node graphs with hidden edge reliabilities. The agent sees only its node label, its number of
exits and the goal. In task E5, six edges are blocked in turn during a run, and each block reopens with probability
0.1 (RARE), 0.5 (HALF) or 0.9 (OFTEN).

The owner's adopted agent (AD-13, "BELIEF") is a value-iteration planner on world model v6. That model keeps a
two-regime belief about each exit it has seen change, with ρ fixed at 1/2 and hazard h = 0.01. It was developed and
gated (CF-06) on one generator, the development family. The baseline, MBVI3, is the same planner on model v3, with
change detection but no belief about reversal.

**M13 asks:** does BELIEF keep its advantage over MBVI3, with nothing tuned, on a family of worlds structurally unlike
the development family that no agent had seen? The family is modular rings: 4 modules of 6 nodes, a ring of single
bridges, degree at most 4. There are 165 fresh graphs, frozen as a bank before any agent ran.

**The recorded answer** is NET ADVANTAGE RETAINED ON THE MODULAR FAMILY, WITHIN THE SAFETY MARGIN. All five lines
pass.

## The claims under review

From [RESULTS.md](../RESULTS.md):

| | Claim | Where |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | The five verdict lines and the summary statement: VALID; net advantage RETAINED, +125.4 [92.0, 160.1]; SAFE, RARE cost +9.8 [3.6, 14.9] against a margin of 25; recovery SHOWN, +65.1 [50.9, 79.9]; behavioural mechanism SHOWN, +1.461 [1.255, 1.673] | header, §2 |
| C2 | "The belief kept its advantage over MBVI3 on one held-out structural family, in one development-level run" | header, §12 |
| C3 | "It recovers reopened routes faster on this family" (permitted by SPEC rule 3: recovery SHOWN and the OFTEN attribution GENUINE) | header, §12 |
| C4 | "Its belief changes what it does after meeting a block" (permitted by rule 3: G4 SHOWN) | header |
| C5 | Attribution: OFTEN GENUINE REVERSALS, mix GENUINE REVERSALS AND FALSE-ALARM REPAIR; the split is exact | §5 |
| C6 | Descriptive, written after the result: 97% of the OFTEN recovery difference lies in labels where only BELIEF re-crossed a reopened edge in its own window, the reverse of M11. "Recovers faster" here "largely is going back to the reopened route sooner" | §8 |
| C7 | The limitations (§11) and disclosures (§10) are complete enough for the claims made | §10–11 |

## Questions

Please answer each with a verdict (sound / sound with caveats / weak / wrong), one to three sentences citing a file
or appendix, and a classification (MATERIAL / MINOR / NOTE) for anything you would change.

1. **Implementation against the specification.** Do `run_m13.mjs`, `m13_lib.mjs` (`analyse`, `readingRule`) and
   `measures.mjs` compute exactly the criteria, the bootstrap seeds, the verdict lines and the summary that SPEC.md
   defines?
2. **Ground-truth isolation.** Is there any path by which the task's ground truth (`context.isBlockedPair`, the
   schedule, edge reliabilities) reaches BELIEF or MBVI3? The diagnostic arms receive it, by design, only as
   `archiveIf` (`cognitive/testbed/arms_v6.mjs`).
3. **Held-out status and the choice of family.** The family was chosen before any agent ran on it, from structure
   alone. But the choice used knowledge from M11 and M12 (self-identified point 1 below). Is "one held-out structural
   family" a fair description? Does the choice bias the comparison in a way the record should state more strongly?
4. **Novelty.** Is the pre-declared novelty check adequate to call the family structurally different? It requires
   the bank's 5th-percentile clustering to exceed the development family's 95th percentile, and cyclomatic number 13
   against 7.
5. **Statistics.**
   - Per-label paired percentile bootstrap (10,000 resamples) on skewed per-label sums: for G1, SD 223 and median 44.
   - n = 165, chosen by power for d = 0.22.
   - Uncorrected secondary criteria.
   - Summing three families per label.

   Is anything mishandled?
6. **Safety (G2).** BELIEF uses more steps than MBVI3 in 133 of 165 RARE labels. The margin of 25 is inherited from
   CF-06. Is "SAFE", and the wording around it, defensible?
7. **The G4 construct.** G4 counts reopened edges an agent met while blocked and crossed again, at any time. It is a
   count, not a rate. BELIEF also meets more blocks (3.32 against 2.31 a run in OFTEN). Does G4 support C4 as worded?
8. **Attribution.** Are A1–A5 and the exactness check computed as specified? Are C5 and its stated limits correct?
9. **Post-result analyses** (RESULTS §8 and §9 and their scripts). Are they computed correctly, labelled as
   post-result, and worded within what they show? Does C6 overreach?
10. **The independent check** (`results/check/verify_m13.mjs`), written before the result existed. Is it independent
    enough to support "all checks pass"? Did it check what RESULTS §1 says it checked?
11. **Provenance and integrity:** hashes, pins, determinism, the clean-tree rule, the wake request and standby log,
    and the disclosed looks before the freeze. Is anything missing or inconsistent?
12. **Conclusions.** Are RESULTS §12 and the PROJECT_STATE wording supported? Is there anything to withdraw or narrow?
13. **Overall:** SOUND / SOUND WITH CORRECTIONS / NOT SOUND. List the MATERIAL findings first.

## Self-identified points

The designer lists these so that the review need not rediscover them. None changed a verdict line under the frozen
rules. Whether any should change the wording is for the reviewer.

1. **The family was chosen with mechanism knowledge.** PROPOSAL.md §2 chose the modular ring partly as
   "mechanism-relevant": a heavier tail of long detours (11% of blocks costing 4+ steps, against 2% in development).
   M12 had found that the gain comes from going back to a written-off route.
   - The perturbed lattice was rejected because its zero-detour share (56%) resembled the app graph's (70%), where
     M11's effects were small, and because it skipped 19 events.
   - No agent ran on either family before the freeze.
   - So the family is held out from development, but it was not drawn at random. It was chosen as structurally
     different and testable, in a direction that M12's account predicts favours BELIEF.
   - RESULTS §11.1–2 state "one family" and "descriptors move together". They do not state this selection.
2. **Same author.** The verifier, the driver and the analyses have one author. The verifier is independent in code
   path only. It shares `pairedBootstrap` (for the interval seeds), `planE5` (the schedule) and the world builder.
3. **G4's raw inputs.** `met` and `crossedLater` are computed from the environment's step log during the run. That
   log is not stored for the 1,962 untraced runs.
   - The pre-result verifier checks those measures for internal consistency, and their own-window parts against the
     task's own counts. It cannot rebuild `met` or `crossedLater`.
   - This package adds a supplementary check, written after the result:
     [check_measures_from_traces.mjs](check_measures_from_traces.mjs). It rebuilds every event's measures in the 18
     traced runs from the recorder's separate step log: all 108 events agree field by field, with 782 ground-truth
     window checks.
   - Run B recomputed the same measures in the 990 core runs, untraced, and matched exactly. That shows determinism,
     not independence.
4. **G4 is a count conditional on meeting.** The per-run conditional rate (met and returned / met) is about 59% for
   BELIEF and 21% for MBVI3 in OFTEN: 1.95 / 3.32 and 0.49 / 2.31, from RESULTS §4. That ratio of means is not a
   pre-declared statistic.
5. **HALF is carried by fewer, larger differences.** Its median is −1, and 84 labels favour MBVI3 against 76 for
   BELIEF. G1 sums the families and is CONSISTENT, but the top decile of labels supplies 51.7% of G1's sum.
6. **The RARE cost is real.** BELIEF is worse in 133 of 165 labels. "SAFE" is relative to CF-06's margin only.
7. **Post-result material.** RESULTS §7 to §9, their scripts and this package's two supplementary scripts were all
   written after the result. The pre-declared illustration (§9) shows no return by either agent. It is reported as it
   came out.
8. **Step scales differ.** M13's runs are about twice as long as M11's. The cross-study raw comparisons in §7 are rough.
   The standardized effect is the only like-for-like number.
9. **Two descriptive checks were adapted from M11 after seeing the result:** the crossing split (§8) and the trace
   summary (§9). §8's two groupings (own window, any time) were both printed and reported.
10. **Diagnostic capped episodes.** BELIEF_REALONLY had one capped episode in RARE and one in OFTEN. No criterion uses
    it. The core arms had none.
11. **The bootstrap tags.** G2 and G4 use the same tags as the per-family table rows for those contrasts. G1 and G3
    have their own. The G3 interval therefore differs from the family-table OFTEN `afterReversed` interval in the
    second decimal.
12. **The proposal's development 95th percentile.** The proposal wrote 0.19, from 60 survey labels. The frozen check
    uses 0.167, computed on the 165 development labels. PROPOSAL.md's status line discloses this.

## How to verify

Run these from a clone at the package commit; `git log -1 -- experiments/m13_generalization/review` gives the commit.
Use Node 24, as the run used v24.15.0. Line endings:
- `experiments/m13_generalization/**` is stored byte-exact (`-text` in `.gitattributes`).
- The pinned agent code under `cognitive/` is hashed by content, with CRLF read as LF (`pins.mjs`).

| Step | Command | Expect |
|---|---|---|
| Frozen hashes | `cd experiments/m13_generalization && sha256sum -c SPEC.sha256` | 4 OK |
| Pins | `node experiments/m13_generalization/pins.mjs --check` | exit 0 (44 files) |
| Bank and structure regenerate | `node experiments/m13_generalization/make_bank.mjs --check` | exit 0 |
| Result files | `cd experiments/m13_generalization/results && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` | 13 OK |
| Pre-result independent check | `node experiments/m13_generalization/results/check/verify_m13.mjs .` | "all checks pass" |
| G4 inputs from the recorder log | `node experiments/m13_generalization/review/check_measures_from_traces.mjs experiments/m13_generalization/results/m13_result.json` | "all agree" |
| Per-label table | `node experiments/m13_generalization/review/per_label.mjs experiments/m13_generalization/results/m13_result.json \| diff - experiments/m13_generalization/review/per_label.csv` | no output |
| Descriptive scripts | `node experiments/m13_generalization/results/check/crossing_split.mjs experiments/m13_generalization/results/m13_result.json \| diff - experiments/m13_generalization/results/check/crossing_split.out` (likewise `trace_summary.mjs`) | no output |
| Regression tests | `node scripts/test.mjs` (about 4 minutes; agents run only on development-family worlds) | 195/195 |
| The bundle | `node experiments/m13_generalization/review/build_bundle.mjs`, then `git diff --exit-code` | the same file; no diff |

**A full re-execution** would re-run the spent labels. That needs the owner's approval, and it takes about 2.5 hours
in a scratch clone. The driver refuses while `results/m13_result.json` exists, so it must be removed in the clone.
The run is deterministic: run A and run B core rows hash to `86f0bd3f…a608e02d`.

**Bootstrap seeds** (`pairedBootstrap(diffs, tag)`, `cognitive/testbed/stats.mjs`):

| Criterion or contrast | Tag |
|---|---|
| G1 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|G1\|mix` |
| G2 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|RARE\|cost` |
| G3 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|G3\|OFTEN\|afterReversed` |
| G4 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|OFTEN\|metAndReturned` |
| A1 / A2 / A3 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|attr\|OFTEN\|real` / `…\|realAfter` / `…\|false` |
| A4 / A5 | `m13\|stats\|m13\|attr\|mix\|real` / `…\|mix\|false` |
| Per-family table | `m13\|stats\|m13\|<family>\|<contrast>` |

[per_label.csv](per_label.csv) holds every per-label difference these are computed from.

and `traces`, verbatim, plus one example row. The full file is in the repository, hash-checked by `SHA256SUMS`.

## Diagnostic arms and attribution: where the definitions are

- **Definition** (SPEC.md, "Attribution"): A1–A5, the "holds" rule (mean > 0 and lower bound > 0), the labels, and
  the exactness check.
- **The arms** (`cognitive/testbed/arms_v6.mjs`, `armAgentV6`): BELIEF with exactly its parameters, plus
  `archiveIf(pairKey)`, a predicate derived from the task context.
  - BELIEF_REALONLY archives an exit that its change detector declares worse only if the task has that edge blocked
    at that moment: a genuine change, reversing or permanent.
  - BELIEF_FALSEONLY archives it only if the edge is open at that moment: a false alarm.
  - Each declaration is classified once, by the state of the world when it is made.
  - The predicate's only consumer is world model v6's archive step (`cognitive/mind/v6/world_model.mjs`,
    `mayArchive`; every exit may be archived when `archiveIf` is absent).
- **Construction** (`m13_lib.mjs` lines 46–49, `ARMS`): BELIEF is built by `makeAdoptedAgent(env, label)` and MBVI3
  by `makeAgentForV6('MBVI3', env, label, {})`. Neither builder takes the context; `runOne` (line 62) passes it as an
  unused third argument.
- **The computation:**
  - the driver: `m13_lib.mjs` lines 172–185, inside `analyse`: contrasts A1–A5, the exactness flag and the
    `gainSource` labels;
  - the independent recomputation: `results/check/verify_m13.mjs`, lines 105–119;
  - the per-label values: `per_label.csv`, columns `A1_…` to `A5_…`.
- **The exactness inputs:** each row's `diagnostics.alarms.opensFalse` / `opensReal`, from task E5's `diagnoseE5`
  (`cognitive/testbed/task_e5.mjs`).
- **Tests:** `cognitive/tests/test_m13_generalization.mjs`, ARMS and PASSIVE.

## Appendix A. Frozen specification and its recorded hashes

### experiments/m13_generalization/SPEC.md

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/SPEC.md` · SHA-256 (content) `d4140479e470f2d5b1e876de6644e8024bbc540c410d0cbfdb5957b932b6c430` · 21931 bytes · 370 lines

````markdown
# M13 — cognitive generalization: the adopted belief on a held-out modular-ring family

**Status.** Frozen, 2026-11-18, on the owner's word "Freeze and run", at revision 2.
- **Built** on the owner's word "Approve with diagnostics", after [PROPOSAL.md](PROPOSAL.md).
- **Revision 2** makes the owner's three corrections: the verdict hierarchy, power and exclusions, and diagnostic
  attribution.
- **Approved** by the owner, together with the driver's alignment to it ("Approve both"). The reading rule now writes
  the five lines and the summary statement, and G0 requires no exclusion.

The driver refuses to run the study unless this line begins "Frozen" and every line of `SPEC.sha256` matches.

**Name.** "M13" is the owner's name for this milestone. Its folder is `experiments/m13_generalization/` because
`experiments/m13/` holds an earlier, unrelated record guarded by its own verifier.

## Question

Does the adopted AD-13 belief keep its advantages over MBVI3 when it is moved, with nothing tuned, to a family of
worlds that is structurally different from the one it was developed on and that no agent has seen?

This is a development-level study. It decides nothing about AD-13 by itself.

## What runs

Every part the agents and the task use is existing code, used unchanged.

| Part | What it is |
|---|---|
| **Worlds** | The modular-ring family ([`worlds_modular.mjs`](worlds_modular.mjs)): 4 modules of 6 nodes; inside each module a random spanning tree plus random edges up to 8; one bridge from each module to the next, in a ring; degree at most 4. A new graph per label. The 165 graphs are frozen in `graph_bank.json`. The testbed's hidden relabelling is applied by an exact copy of its `finish()`, proven identical by a test |
| **Task** | E5 (`cognitive/testbed/task_e5.mjs`), parameters as frozen for CF-06: 600 exploration steps in 50-step episodes, 30 training episodes, 6 events each with 10 blocked and 10 after episodes, cap 200, start at least 3 hops from the goal. Each block reopens with probability 0.1 (RARE), 0.5 (HALF) or 0.9 (OFTEN). Edge reliabilities 0.5–1 per label |
| **BELIEF** | `makeAdoptedAgent` (AD-13): model v6, ρ fixed at 1/2, h = 0.01, every other parameter at its default |
| **MBVI3** | The registry's MBVI3, the baseline |
| **BELIEF_REALONLY / BELIEF_FALSEONLY** | CF-06's privileged diagnostic arms (`arms_v6.mjs`): BELIEF with exactly its parameters, archiving only genuine changes / only false alarms, by ground truth. Secondary; in no criterion |
| **Behaviour measures** | [`measures.mjs`](measures.mjs), from the environment's own step log (`GraphEnv.trace()`), the same for every arm. Checked in every run against the task's own counts |
| **Recorder** | `experiments/m11_routes/trace.mjs`, passive, on labels 0–2 for the core arms |

## Fixed in advance

- **Labels:** `m13|modular|0` … `m13|modular|164` (165), in each of the three families, for all four arms. The driver
  refuses any label in a namespace used before (`cf|`, `mfw|`, `m13survey|`, `m13test|`, `m13smoke|`,
  `m13|structure|`).
- **Sample size:** 165 labels, all of them analysed (see "Sample size and power").
- **Worlds:** `graph_bank.json`, pinned by hash. It is regenerated and compared before the run.
- **Structural record and novelty check** (`structure.json`, from the graphs and the schedules alone; no agent):

  | | Development family (`cf|dev|world|0..164`) | App graph | **Bank** |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Nodes / edges / cyclomatic number | 24 / 30 / 7 | 20 / 39 / 20 | **24 / 36 / 13** |
  | Clustering: mean [5th, 95th percentile] | 0.053 [0.000, 0.167] | 0.075 | **0.355 [0.243, 0.464]** |
  | Start–goal distance | 4.07 | 3.17 | **4.58** |
  | Blocks costing no detour / a detour of 4+ steps | 29% / 3% | 69% / 0% | **34% / 10%** |
  | Skipped events | 36 of 2,970 | 0 | **1 of 2,970** |

  **Novelty holds:**
  - the bank's 5th-percentile clustering (0.243) is above the development family's 95th percentile (0.167);
  - every bank graph has cyclomatic number 13, and every development graph has 7.
- **Pins:** `PINS.json` holds the SHA-256 of every file the driver executes (44 files: its full static import
  closure, including all agent, model, planner, task, environment and statistics code), the data, and the two scripts
  it runs.
- **Exclusions:** task E5 excludes a (label, family) only when `planE5` finds no start–goal pair at distance 3 or
  more. That is a pure function of the label's graph, so it is computed from the frozen bank before the freeze.
  - The result: **none** of the 495 (label, family) pairs is excluded (`power/power_m13.out`, section 0).
  - The analysed sample is therefore fixed now: all 165 labels, in all three families.
  - No label is ever replaced or added.
  - Any exclusion at run time would mean the code or the bank had changed despite the pins, and makes the run INVALID
    (G0).
  - Skipped events (1 of 2,970 in the bank) do not exclude anything; they are reported.
- **Statistics:** per-label paired differences; mean and 95% paired percentile bootstrap (10,000 resamples, seeded by
  label, `pairedBootstrap`); the median and the number of labels each way. Inequalities are strict, and an empty
  contrast never passes.

## Criteria

Each criterion is a per-label paired difference, with its mean and its 95% paired percentile bootstrap interval
[lower, upper]. Each is decided on its own; no criterion's result changes another's.

| | Role | Criterion | Statistic | Passes when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G0** | Validity gate | Validity | Core rows of run A and run B identical; `node scripts/test.mjs` passes; the novelty check holds; no (label, family) excluded | all four |
| **G1** | **Primary** | Net advantage | Per label, the sum over RARE, HALF and OFTEN of steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF) over the change phase | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |
| **G2** | **Safety** | Permanent-change safety | RARE, per label, steps(BELIEF) − steps(MBVI3) | upper < 25 (CF-06's B2 margin) |
| **G3** | Secondary | Recovery | OFTEN, per label, steps in the 10 episodes after each reopening, MBVI3 − BELIEF | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |
| **G4** | Secondary | Behavioural mechanism | OFTEN, per label, the number of reopened edges the agent tried while they were blocked and crossed again after the reopening (at any time), BELIEF − MBVI3 | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |

## Verdict

The result is reported as **five independent lines**, one per criterion. Then comes **one summary statement**, built
from G0, G1 and G2 only.

| Line | Value | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| **Validity** (G0) | VALID | all four parts hold |
| | INVALID (*failing parts*) | otherwise; no other line is then interpreted, though all are printed |
| **Net advantage** (G1, primary) | RETAINED | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |
| | REVERSED | upper < 0 (BELIEF takes more steps than MBVI3) |
| | NOT SHOWN | otherwise (the interval contains 0) |
| **Safety** (G2) | SAFE | upper < 25 |
| | NOT SHOWN SAFE | upper ≥ 25 |
| **Recovery** (G3, secondary) | SHOWN | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |
| | NOT SHOWN | otherwise |
| **Behavioural mechanism** (G4, secondary) | SHOWN | mean > 0 and lower > 0 |
| | NOT SHOWN | otherwise |

**Summary statement** (the only sentence the record may use about generalization):

| Validity | Net advantage | Safety | Summary statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| INVALID | any | any | **INVALID — NO CRITERION IS INTERPRETED** |
| VALID | RETAINED | SAFE | **NET ADVANTAGE RETAINED ON THE MODULAR FAMILY, WITHIN THE SAFETY MARGIN** |
| VALID | RETAINED | NOT SHOWN SAFE | **NET ADVANTAGE RETAINED ON THE MODULAR FAMILY; SAFETY NOT SHOWN** |
| VALID | NOT SHOWN | any | **NET ADVANTAGE NOT SHOWN ON THE MODULAR FAMILY** |
| VALID | REVERSED | any | **NET ADVANTAGE REVERSED ON THE MODULAR FAMILY** |

**Rules for reading the lines:**
1. **The secondary criteria cannot change the summary.** G3 and G4 never enter it. A G3 or G4 failure does not erase
   a retained net advantage, and a G3 or G4 pass does not create one.
2. **"Generalizes" and "keeps its advantage" are reserved.** They may be written only with the second summary
   statement, and always as "on one held-out structural family, in one development-level run".
3. **Claims about recovery and behaviour need their own line.**
   - "Recovers reopened routes faster" may be written only when Recovery is SHOWN and the OFTEN attribution includes
     GENUINE (§ Attribution), the CF-06 rule.
   - "Its belief changes what it does after meeting a block" may be written only when the behavioural mechanism is
     SHOWN.
   - With a retained net advantage and either of these NOT SHOWN, the record says the net advantage held but that
     account was not shown on this family.
4. **gainShape** is CF-06's rule, reported for G1 and G3 on their own lines. It is **CONSISTENT** if the criterion
   has:
   - more labels in favour than against;
   - a positive median;
   - a positive mean once the most favourable ⌈0.1 n⌉ labels are removed.

   Otherwise it is **TAIL-DRIVEN**, naming each failing part. It never changes a line. With a retained net advantage
   and a TAIL-DRIVEN G1, the summary statement ends with " — FROM A MINORITY OF LABELS", and the record prints the
   per-label differences and the top decile's share.
5. **Multiplicity.** G1 is the single primary test. G2 is a separate safety bound. G3 and G4 are secondary,
   uncorrected, and support no claim beyond their own lines.

## Sample size and power

The full calculation is in `power/power_m13.mjs`, with its output in `power/power_m13.out` and `power_m13.json`. It
reads the stored CF-06 and M11 rows and the frozen bank, and runs no agent.

To reproduce it:

```
node experiments/m13_generalization/power/power_m13.mjs
```

It takes about 4 minutes and is deterministic.

**Model (G1).**
- **The difference:** for label i, dᵢ = Σ over f ∈ {RARE, HALF, OFTEN} of steps_MBVI3(i, f) − steps_BELIEF(i, f). The
  three families are not separate tests: each label contributes one paired difference, their sum (CF-06's B1 unit).
- **Independence:** labels are independent draws. Each has its own graph, layout, schedule, reliabilities and agent
  streams.
- **Correlation between families:** the families of one label share the graph and the layout, so their differences
  may be correlated. The SD used is the SD of the sum, so these covariances are included; no independence between
  families is assumed. The recorded within-label correlations are 0.161, 0.203 and 0.472 in CF-06 (SD of the sum
  190.6, against 153.3 if independent), and −0.08 to 0.05 in M11.
- **Effect size:** the standardized effect is δ = μ/σ of dᵢ. The planning assumption is δ = 0.22, half the smaller
  recorded value (CF-06 0.4442, M11 0.4982; half is 0.2221). No assumption is made about σ itself: power depends
  only on δ.
- **Test:** G1 passes when the 95% paired percentile bootstrap interval lies above 0. Its normal approximation is the
  one-sided z-test at α = 0.025: power(n) = Φ(δ√n − z₀.₉₇₅).

**Calculation.** With z₀.₉₇₅ = 1.959964 and z₀.₈₀ = 0.841621:

n = ⌈((1.959964 + 0.841621) / 0.22)²⌉ = ⌈162.17⌉ = 163.

| δ | n = 116 | n = 163 | **n = 165 (analysed)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.20 | 0.577 | 0.724 | 0.729 |
| **0.22** | 0.659 | 0.802 | **0.807** |
| 0.30 | 0.898 | 0.969 | 0.971 |

The n = 116 column shows the draft's worst case, 10% excluded. That rule is withdrawn.

**Check by simulation.** Each cell is 2,000 simulated studies, each analysed with the study's own `pairedBootstrap`
(10,000 resamples) and the rule "mean > 0 and lower > 0", at δ = 0.22. The power at n = 165 was:

| Shape of dᵢ | Power at n = 165 | Monte Carlo SE |
|---|---|---|
| Normal | 0.806 | 0.009 |
| CF-06's recorded G1 differences (standardized, shifted to δ) | 0.911 | 0.006 |
| M11's recorded G1 differences (standardized, shifted to δ) | 0.846 | 0.008 |

The analytic 0.807 is the planning figure. The recorded skewed shapes give more power, not less.

**The analysed sample.** Exclusions are computed from the frozen bank, and there are none (see "Fixed in advance"). So
the analysed n is 165, power at δ = 0.22 is 0.807, and no label is replaced.

**The other criteria:**
- **G2** passes when its upper bound is below 25.
  - With CF-06's cost and SD (17.40, 27.08), P(pass) at n = 165 is 0.950.
  - With M11's (2.87, 12.80), it is 1.000.
  - With the larger SD, a true cost up to 19.1 steps passes with 80% probability.
- **G3** (secondary): power is 0.807 at δ = 0.22, and above 0.999 at the recorded G3 effects (CF-06 0.626, M11
  0.450).
- **G4** (secondary): no earlier record measured MBVI3's returns, so no spread is known and **no power is claimed**.
  The result reports its minimum detectable effect, (z₀.₉₇₅ + z₀.₈₀) · SD/√n.

**Assumptions, stated:**
- The new family's standardized effect is unknown; 0.22 is the smallest effect the study is designed to detect, not
  a prediction.
- The normal approximation is used for planning; the simulation checks it with the recorded shapes.
- Power refers to G1 only.

## Attribution (diagnostic arms; reported, never in a criterion or a verdict line)

The rule is CF-06's, applied unchanged. It is stated here in full.

**Contrasts** (per label; steps over the change phase unless stated):

| Name | Exact contrast |
|---|---|
| A1 genuine, OFTEN, total | OFTEN, steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF_REALONLY) |
| A2 genuine, OFTEN, after reopenings | OFTEN, steps in the 10 episodes after each reopening, MBVI3 − BELIEF_REALONLY |
| A3 false alarms, OFTEN, total | OFTEN, steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF_FALSEONLY) |
| A4 genuine, mix | Σ over RARE, HALF, OFTEN of steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF_REALONLY) |
| A5 false alarms, mix | Σ over RARE, HALF, OFTEN of steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF_FALSEONLY) |

**How a contrast is decided:**
- **Statistic:** the mean, with the 95% paired percentile bootstrap interval (10,000 resamples, seeded by
  `m13|stats|m13|attr|…`).
- **Threshold:** a contrast **holds** iff mean > 0 and lower > 0. The inequalities are strict, and an empty contrast
  never holds.

**The two attributions:**
- **GENUINE (OFTEN)** iff A1 and A2 both hold. **FALSE (OFTEN)** iff A3 holds.
- **GENUINE (mix)** iff A4 holds. **FALSE (mix)** iff A5 holds.

**The label**, reported separately for OFTEN (`gainSource`) and for the mix (`gainSourceMix`):

| GENUINE | FALSE | Label |
|---|---|---|
| holds | holds | GENUINE REVERSALS AND FALSE-ALARM REPAIR (mixed) |
| holds | does not | GENUINE REVERSALS |
| does not | holds | FALSE-ALARM REPAIR |
| does not | does not | NOT ATTRIBUTED |

**Exactness check.** The split is exact iff, in every label and family:
- BELIEF_REALONLY opened no archive on an exit that was not blocked (`alarms.opensFalse` = 0);
- BELIEF_FALSEONLY opened none on an exit that was blocked (`alarms.opensReal` = 0).

Both counts come from the task's ground-truth diagnostics. If either fails, both labels read **INVALID (the
diagnostic split is not exact)**, and no attribution is interpreted.

**What attribution can and cannot say:**
- The two diagnostic effects need not add up to BELIEF's. No residual is tested.
- Attribution changes no verdict line and no summary statement. Its only use is the wording rule for "recovers
  reopened routes" (§ Verdict, rule 3).
- The diagnostic arms intervene on what the belief archives. They do not show that returning causes the gain.

**Isolation of ground truth:**
- **The ordinary arms never receive ground truth.**
  - BELIEF is built by `makeAdoptedAgent(env, label)` and MBVI3 by `makeAgentForV6('MBVI3', env, label, {})`. Neither
    builder takes the task's context; `runOne` hands it to them as an unused third argument.
  - The context reaches only two other places. The first is the passive recorder, for the core arms' trace labels:
    it reads `context.isBlockedPair` to label its own record and returns nothing to the agent (`act`, `observe` and
    `inspect` pass through; traced and untraced rows are identical).
  - The second is the diagnostic arms' builder.
- **The diagnostic arms stay isolated.**
  - `armAgentV6` (CF-06's `arms_v6.mjs`, unchanged) derives a single predicate, "this exit is blocked now", from the
    context. It passes it only as `archiveIf` into that arm's own model.
  - Every run builds a new environment, agent and model, so nothing passes between arms or runs.
  - The behaviour measures read the environment's step log after the agent's last step.
- **Tested** in `test_m13_generalization.mjs`:
  - ARMS: BELIEF has no `archiveIf`; each diagnostic arm has one and otherwise exactly BELIEF's parameters.
  - PASSIVE: the recorder and the environment reference change no row; `runOne` equals `runE5` with the adopted
    agent.

## Reported, never in a criterion

- **Generalization size:** G1's mean / SD, beside 0.444 (CF-06) and 0.498 (M11).
- **G4's minimum detectable effect:** 2.80 · SD/√n, computed in RESULTS.md from G4's recorded SD and n.
- **Moderator:** per reversing event, the after-window difference by the block's detour excess (0, 1–3, 4+).
  Descriptive. It asks whether M12's observational account carries over; it is not a test of it.
- **Per family and arm:** every measure, the detector counts, BELIEF's archives (genuine reversing, genuine
  permanent, false) and confirmations, returns (own window, later only), and the median steps from reopening to the
  first return.
- **The 18 traces:** BELIEF's and MBVI3's predicted chance that a blocked exit works, over time.

## Integrity

- **Run once,** with `node experiments/m13_generalization/run_m13.mjs --domain m13`. The driver refuses if any of these
  hold:
  - this file is not Frozen;
  - a line of `SPEC.sha256` does not match;
  - a pinned file differs from `PINS.json`;
  - `cognitive/`, `scripts/`, this folder or the recorder has an uncommitted change;
  - the bank or the structural record differs from the generator;
  - a label is not fresh;
  - the novelty check fails;
  - the test suite fails;
  - a result exists.
- **Determinism and passivity.**
  - Run A runs all four arms, with the recorder on the trace labels.
  - Run B repeats the core arms without it.
  - The core rows must be identical.
- **Recorded with the result:**
  - HEAD at launch and at the end;
  - this file's SHA-256 and the pins' SHA-256;
  - the bank's SHA-256;
  - Node version and platform;
  - the test result;
  - the SHA-256 of `main.js`, `index.html`, `connections.json` and `neurons.json` (`main.js` is expected to be
    `c92c5c6b…`, untouched).
- **Nothing is tuned after the run.** `experiments/m13_generalization/RESULTS.md` is written whatever the outcome, and
  an independent check is written before the result is seen.
- **Looks before the freeze (disclosed):**
  - the structure survey of PROPOSAL.md (prototype labels `m13survey|*`, no agent);
  - the structural record above (no agent);
  - the power calculation (`power/`): it reads the stored CF-06 and M11 rows and the frozen bank, runs no agent and
    reads no modular-world outcome;
  - the regression tests, which run agents only on development-family worlds (`m13test|*`);
  - two driver smoke runs on development-family worlds, checked for output structure, determinism and timing only,
    with no measure printed or read:
    - the first on `m13smoke|dev|0` and `1`;
    - the second, after the alignment, on `m13smoke|dev|0`.

  No agent has run on a modular world.

## What it can and cannot show

**It can show:**
- whether the advantage survives a structural shift with nothing tuned;
- whether an agent that met a block comes back to it, measured the same way for both agents in every run;
- from the diagnostic arms, whether the gain needs belief about genuine changes, about false alarms, or both.

**It cannot show:**
- other families or larger worlds;
- which of the shifted descriptors matters (clustering, cycles, route length and detour length move together);
- that returning causes the gain within an agent;
- anything confirmatory: one run, by the designer, on local commits.

## Consequence

None for AD-13 or the agent. The result is recorded. With the summary statement **NET ADVANTAGE RETAINED ON THE
MODULAR FAMILY, WITHIN THE SAFETY MARGIN**, the record may say the belief kept its advantage on one held-out structural
family, in one development-level run. It may not say that the belief generalizes in general.

## Run

**Time:** about 2.5 hours, plus about 4 minutes for the test suite.
- Run A: 165 × 3 × 4 = 1,980 E5 runs.
- Run B: 990 core runs.

In the smoke run (two development-family labels, all traced) a run took 2.7–3.2 s. The machine is kept awake with the same per-process wake request as
CF-06 and M11.

**To freeze:**
1. Set the status line to "**Status.** Frozen", with the date and the owner's word.
2. Write `SPEC.sha256` with lines for SPEC.md, PINS.json, graph_bank.json and structure.json.
3. Commit.
4. Run once.

## Files

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `PROPOSAL.md`, `proposal_inputs/` | The approved proposal and its no-agent inputs |
| `SPEC.md`, `SPEC.sha256` | This specification and its frozen hashes |
| `worlds_modular.mjs` | The generator, the world builder and the bank |
| `graph_bank.json`, `structure.json`, `make_bank.mjs` | The frozen worlds, their structural record, and the script that writes and checks both |
| `structure.mjs` | Descriptors, novelty check, detour excess |
| `measures.mjs` | Behaviour measures for every arm |
| `m13_lib.mjs` | Configuration, arms, one run, analysis, reading rule |
| `pins.mjs`, `PINS.json` | The pinned files and their hashes |
| `power/power_m13.mjs` → `power_m13.out`, `power_m13.json` | The sample-size and power calculation, and the exclusions computed from the bank |
| `run_m13.mjs` | The driver and its refusal checks |
| `cognitive/tests/test_m13_generalization.mjs` | Regression tests (in `npm test`) |
````

## Appendix B. The approved proposal and its no-agent inputs

### experiments/m13_generalization/PROPOSAL.md

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/PROPOSAL.md` · SHA-256 (content) `5a041937c6ef90e6289c8839007bc09c0159d56d1d0c6d1eec448075823a45f8` · 15579 bytes · 234 lines

````markdown
# M13 — Cognitive generalization: does the adopted belief keep its advantage in an unseen kind of world?

**Status.** Proposal, 2026-11-18. **Approved the same day on the owner's word "Approve with diagnostics"**, and built
as [SPEC.md](SPEC.md) (a draft until the owner approves the freeze). The text below is the proposal as approved. One
value differs in the build: over 165 development labels instead of the survey's 60, the development family's
95th-percentile clustering is 0.167, not 0.19. The novelty rule is unchanged.

**The inputs it rests on, both computed without running any agent:**
- **A structure-only survey of graphs.** It reads the graphs and the E5 schedule ([proposal_inputs/structure_survey.mjs](proposal_inputs/structure_survey.mjs),
  [`.out`](proposal_inputs/structure_survey.out)).
- **The per-label spread of the stored CF-06 and M11 rows** ([proposal_inputs/power_inputs.mjs](proposal_inputs/power_inputs.mjs),
  [`.out`](proposal_inputs/power_inputs.out)).

**Name.** "M13" is the owner's name for the milestone. The folder is `experiments/m13_generalization/` because
`experiments/m13/` is a legacy record guarded by its own verifier.

## Recommendation

Run the adopted agent (AD-13) and MBVI3, both unchanged, once, on a **held-out family of modular-ring worlds**.

- **Never used before:** the family appears nowhere in development or in any gate.
- **A new graph for every label:** the topology itself varies across labels.
- **Structurally outside the development family:** measured from the graphs alone, before any agent sees them.

**The run:**
- **Task:** E5, exactly as frozen for CF-06 and M11.
- **Labels:** 165 fresh labels × 3 reversal families (RARE, HALF, OFTEN).
- **Criteria:** four pre-registered criteria, G1–G4 (§6), with the CF-06 margins unchanged.
- **Measurement:** returns to reopened routes are measured for **both** agents in **every** run. M12 could see MBVI3's
  side only in 18 traces; this closes that gap prospectively.
- **Secondary block:** CF-06's two existing privileged diagnostic arms. They turn M12's observational split
  (reopened edges against false alarms) into an intervention on what the belief acts on.
- **Build:** one generator, one measure module, one driver and one test file.
- **Run time:** about 2 hours, once.

## 1. What exists (question 1)

| Part | Where | Use in M13 |
|---|---|---|
| Random worlds: a spanning tree plus random extra edges, maximum degree 4 (24 nodes, 30 edges by default) | `cognitive/testbed/worlds.mjs` `generateWorld` | The **development family**. Every CF stage, the CF-06 gate and every test used it with these defaults. Only labels varied |
| The app graph (20 nodes, 39 edges) | `worlds.mjs` `mfwWorld` | M11's single fixed graph |
| Hidden relabelling of nodes and exits | `worlds.mjs` `finish` (not exported) | Reused exactly: an M13 copy, proven identical by a test (§7). No edit to the testbed |
| Task E5 (six blocks per run, reopening with probability 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.9; the schedule depends only on the label and the true graph) | `cognitive/testbed/task_e5.mjs` | Unchanged; parameters as frozen |
| GraphEnv (`env.trace()` is a runner-only, ground-truth step log) | `cognitive/testbed/env.mjs` | Gives every run's step-level behaviour for both agents. No agent wrapper is needed |
| The adopted agent | `cognitive/testbed/adopted_agent.mjs`, pinned by `test_adopted_agent.mjs` | BELIEF, unchanged |
| MBVI3 | `cognitive/testbed/registry_v6.mjs` | The baseline, unchanged |
| Privileged diagnostic arms `BELIEF_REALONLY` / `BELIEF_FALSEONLY` (the adopted agent, archiving only genuine changes / only false alarms) | `cognitive/testbed/arms_v6.mjs`, as defined in `experiments/cf06/run_gate.mjs` | Secondary, never in a criterion |
| Paired percentile bootstrap, seeded by label | `cognitive/testbed/stats.mjs` | Unchanged |
| Driver pattern (refusals, single run, determinism check, wake hold) and passive recorder | `experiments/m11_routes/` | Copied in pattern; the recorder is reused for 18 pre-declared traces |

## 2. Generalization, not re-seeding (question 2)

| Level | What is new | Instance in this programme |
|---|---|---|
| Re-seed (in-distribution) | Labels only. Same generator, same structure | CF-06: development labels → gate labels |
| One new graph | One topology. Labels vary start, goal and schedule | M11: the app graph |
| **New structural family (M13)** | A generator never used in development. Topology varies per label. Its structure lies outside the development range on pre-declared descriptors | The modular ring |

**The structure survey** (60 labels per row; mean, with 5th–95th percentiles; no agent):

| Descriptor | Development family | App graph (M11) | **Modular ring (proposed)** | Perturbed lattice (rejected) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Nodes / edges | 24 / 30 | 20 / 39 | **24 / 36** | 24 / 32 |
| Cyclomatic number (independent cycles) | 7 | 20 | **13** | 9 |
| Clustering | 0.06 [0.00, 0.19] | 0.08 | **0.34 [0.26, 0.45]** | 0.00 |
| Start–goal distance | 3.97 [3, 6] | 3.15 | **4.98 [3, 8]** | 4.67 |
| Labels with a single shortest route | 73% | 18% | **50%** | 37% |
| Blocks that cost no detour | 26% | 70% | **35%** | 56% |
| Blocks costing a detour of 4 or more steps | 2% | 0% | **11%** | 3% |
| Skipped events | 6 of 720 | 0 | **0** | 19 |

The survey also helps read M11. In the app graph, 70% of the scheduled blocks cost no detour at all, which fits
M11's small effects and many ties.

**Why the modular ring:**
- **Same size and action space as development.** Nodes (24) and the maximum degree (4) match the development family,
  so neither drives a difference.
- **Different structure.** Four modules of six nodes, densely connected inside and joined in a ring by single
  bridges. This gives high clustering, bottlenecks, longer routes and a five-fold heavier tail of long detours.
- **Mechanism-relevant.** M12 (observational) found the gain comes from going back to a written-off route. The
  pay-off grows with the detour avoided, and the timing depends on how long episodes are. Both differ here.

**The perturbed lattice is not chosen**, for structural reasons only. Its regime resembles the app graph's (56% of
blocks cost no detour), and it skips 19 scheduled events.

**Fixed generator parameters**, not to be changed after this proposal:
- 4 modules × 6 nodes;
- each module a random spanning tree plus random edges up to 8 edges;
- one bridge between consecutive modules, between random nodes of degree below 4;
- maximum degree 4.

**Novelty check (part of G0).** It is computed on the frozen graph bank before any agent runs, and must hold:
- the bank's 5th-percentile clustering exceeds the development family's 95th percentile (0.19);
- the cyclomatic number is 13 against the development family's 7.

**Can the adopted agent run there unchanged?** Yes. An agent sees only a node label, its number of exits and the goal
label. Nothing in v6, v3 or the planner depends on topology or size.

## 3. Preventing tuning and leakage (question 3)

1. **The agent is pinned.**
   - `ADOPTED_AGENT` and its existing pin test apply.
   - The specification lists the SHA-256 of every file the agents import, plus task E5, the environment, the runner,
     the RNG and the statistics.
   - The driver refuses to run on any mismatch.
2. **The test worlds are fixed before any agent sees them.**
   - The generator writes a graph bank (edge lists for all 165 labels), whose hash is frozen with the specification.
   - Its structural descriptors are computed and frozen with it.
3. **No agent runs on the modular family before the single run.**
   - The driver's smoke run uses development-family labels in a smoke namespace.
   - The regression tests check the generator's structure only.
   - The survey above used prototype labels `m13survey|*`, ran no agent, and those labels are barred from M13.
4. **Fresh labels.** `m13|modular|0` … `m13|modular|164`. The driver refuses any label in a namespace already used:
   `cf|dev`, `cf|gate*`, `mfw|m11*`, `m13survey`, the test namespaces.
5. **Nothing is chosen from outcomes.**
   - E5's parameters are CF-06's, and the RARE margin is CF-06's 25.
   - The criteria's directions come from CF-06.
   - n comes from recorded variance (§6).
   - M12's findings motivate G4 and one descriptive moderator. They are treated as hypotheses, not as established
     causes.
6. **Run once,** reported whatever the outcome. A result file that already exists blocks the run.

## 4. The comparison (question 4)

| Arm | Definition | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **BELIEF** | `makeAdoptedAgent`: model v6, ρ fixed at 1/2, h = 0.01 | Primary |
| **MBVI3** | The registry's MBVI3 | Baseline |
| BELIEF_REALONLY | BELIEF, archiving only genuine changes (ground truth) | Secondary, privileged, in no criterion |
| BELIEF_FALSEONLY | BELIEF, archiving only false alarms (ground truth) | Secondary, privileged, in no criterion |

The two diagnostic arms are recommended because they are the only way to test M12's split by intervention without
changing an agent. They cost about 40 minutes of extra run time. Dropping them changes nothing in G0–G4.

## 5. Measures (question 5)

All of these come from the E5 rows and from `env.trace()` for every run, computed inside the driver. Nothing is
inferred.

| Purpose | Measure |
|---|---|
| Net effect | Steps over the change phase; per label, the sum over the three reversal families (CF-06 B1's unit) |
| Recovery | OFTEN, steps in the 10 episodes after each reopening |
| Permanent-change safety | RARE extra steps (BELIEF − MBVI3); RARE steps after permanent blocks; futile re-tests of permanent blocks, both arms |
| Belief updating | BELIEF's archives opened (genuine reversing, genuine permanent, false) and reversals confirmed (genuine, false), from its own log; both arms' change-detector counts |
| From belief to behaviour | For each reopened edge, both arms: whether the agent met it while blocked; whether it crossed it again after the reopening, in its own after-window (M11's count) or later (M12's correction); and the steps from reopening to the first return |
| Generalization | The standardized effect (mean / SD) of G1, beside CF-06's 0.44 on development-family worlds and M11's 0.50 on the app graph; gainShape as defined in CF-06 |
| Moderator (descriptive, pre-declared) | Per-event gain against the event's detour excess. If M12's account carries over, the gain should rise with it |
| Belief trajectories | The passive recorder on labels 0–2 (18 traces), as in M11 |

## 6. Labels, sample size and pre-registered criteria (question 6)

**Sample size.** The recorded standardized effects of the net (mix) advantage over MBVI3 are:
- 0.44 in CF-06 (+84.7, SD 190.6);
- 0.50 in M11 (+27.7, SD 55.7).

The recovery effects are 0.63 and 0.45.

- **Smallest effect of interest:** half the smaller net effect, d = 0.22. A paired two-sided 95% interval excluding 0
  with 80% power needs n = (2.80 / 0.22)² = 163, so **n = 165 labels**, giving 97% power at d = 0.30.
- **For G2:** with CF-06's largest RARE spread (SD 27.1), the interval's half-width is about 4.1 steps. G2 therefore
  passes if the true extra cost is below about 21.
- **For G4:** the size of the return measure for MBVI3 has never been measured. Its power is unknown and stated as
  such.

**Exclusions:** a (label, family) whose schedule cannot be laid out, decided from the graph before any agent acts (the
survey needed none).

**Criteria** (per-label paired differences; mean and 95% paired percentile bootstrap, 10,000 resamples, seeded by
label; strict inequalities):

| | Criterion | Statistic | Passes when |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G0** | Validity | Run A = run B rows; all tests pass; novelty check holds; exclusions ≤ 10% | all |
| **G1** | Net advantage retained (primary) | Per label, sum over RARE + HALF + OFTEN of steps(MBVI3) − steps(BELIEF) | mean > 0 and lower bound > 0 |
| **G2** | Permanent-change safety | RARE, steps(BELIEF) − steps(MBVI3) | upper bound < 25 |
| **G3** | Recovery | OFTEN, after-reopening steps, MBVI3 − BELIEF | mean > 0 and lower bound > 0 |
| **G4** | Belief changes behaviour | OFTEN, per label, the number of reopened edges the agent met while blocked and crossed again after the reopening (at any time), BELIEF − MBVI3 | mean > 0 and lower bound > 0 |

**Verdict:**

| Verdict | Condition |
|---|---|
| **GENERALIZES TO THE MODULAR FAMILY** | G0–G4 hold. gainShape (CONSISTENT / TAIL-DRIVEN) is reported on the same line |
| **GENERALIZES IN PART (…)** | G0 and G1 hold; the failing criteria are named |
| **DOES NOT GENERALIZE** (**— HARMFUL** if G1's upper bound < 0) | G0 holds; G1 fails |
| **INVALID** | G0 fails |

**What it can show:** whether the advantage survives a structural shift with nothing tuned, and whether the agent
that met a block comes back to it, for both agents and in every run. With the diagnostic arms, it can also show
whether the gain needs belief about genuine changes, about false alarms, or both.

**What it cannot show:**
- other families, or larger worlds;
- which of the shifted descriptors matters (they move together);
- that returning causes the gain within an agent (the diagnostic arms intervene on what is archived, not on returning);
- anything confirmatory: one run by the designer, on local commits.

## 7. Minimum implementation and verification (question 7)

**New files**, in `experiments/m13_generalization/` unless stated:

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `worlds_modular.mjs` | The generator (the survey prototype, fixed as above); a world builder that is an exact copy of `finish`; the graph-bank writer |
| `graph_bank.json` + its hash | 165 edge lists, frozen with the specification |
| `structure.mjs` | The descriptors and the novelty check (the survey code) |
| `measures.mjs` | Return measures from `env.trace()` and the schedule, for any arm |
| `m13_lib.mjs` | Configuration, the four arms, one run (keeps the environment's reference to read its trace), analysis, reading rule |
| `run_m13.mjs` | The driver. It refuses unless the specification is frozen and hash-matched, the agent and task file hashes match, the tree is clean, the labels are fresh and no result exists |
| `SPEC.md`, `SPEC.sha256` | The frozen specification |
| `cognitive/tests/test_m13_generalization.mjs` | Added to `npm test`. Tests: generator determinism and bank hash; the builder identical to `generateWorld` / `mfwWorld` on the same edges; return measures on hand-made traces; reading rule on synthetic inputs; namespace refusal; keeping the environment reference leaves rows unchanged |
| `.gitattributes` | `experiments/m13_generalization/** -text` (the legacy `experiments/m13/` is not covered) |

**Nothing else changes:** no agent, model, planner, task, environment, `main.js` or browser file.

**Verification:**
- `npm test` all pass;
- legacy gate 14/14;
- the CF-06, M11 and M12 hashes unchanged;
- an independent check written before the result, which recomputes every criterion from the rows;
- run A (all arms) against run B (core arms) identical;
- the system event log checked for standby;
- `RESULTS.md` written whatever the outcome.

**Steps after your decision:**
1. Build and test, then commit locally.
2. Freeze on your word.
3. Run once: about 2 hours. Run A takes about 1,980 E5 runs at about 2.3 s each; run B repeats the 990 core runs.
4. Verify, record and commit locally. No push without your word.

## Decision needed

Approve M13 as proposed (with or without the two diagnostic arms), or name a change.
````

### experiments/m13_generalization/proposal_inputs/structure_survey.mjs

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/proposal_inputs/structure_survey.mjs` · SHA-256 (content) `31623b46fa89e19c7d48824dbf561ad993b4459ff8ff6c1c335f6c7950b6d572` · 6957 bytes · 84 lines

````js
// M13 proposal input: STRUCTURE ONLY. No agent is built or run; no environment is stepped.
// For the development generator (generateWorld defaults, the dev labels already used in development), the app graph,
// and two candidate held-out families (prototype generators, scratch only), it computes per label the graph
// descriptors and E5's schedule descriptors from planE5 (which reads the true graph only):
//   n, m, cyclomatic number, diameter, clustering, bridges share, S-G distance, number of shortest S-G routes,
//   detour excess of each scheduled block (shortest S-G distance with the block minus without), skipped events.
// Labels: cf|dev|world|i (dev, structure only) and m13survey|<family>|i (scratch, never to be used as M13 labels).
import path from 'node:path';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
const ROOT = process.argv[2];
const imp = (p) => import(pathToFileURL(path.join(ROOT, p)).href);
const { generateWorld, mfwWorld, distancesTo, edgeKey } = await imp('cognitive/testbed/worlds.mjs');
const { planE5 } = await imp('cognitive/testbed/task_e5.mjs');
const { makeStream, randInt, shuffled } = await imp('cognitive/testbed/rng.mjs');

const plain = (n, list) => { const adj = Array.from({ length: n }, () => []); for (const [u, v] of list) { adj[u].push(v); adj[v].push(u); } adj.forEach((a) => a.sort((x, y) => x - y)); return { n, edges: list.map(([u, v]) => (u < v ? [u, v] : [v, u])), adj }; };
const connected = (w) => distancesTo(w, 0).every((x) => x >= 0);

// candidate A: ring of 4 modules x 6 nodes; each module a random tree plus extra edges to 8 edges; one bridge between
// consecutive modules (a ring); degree <= 4
function modular(label) {
  const rnd = makeStream(`${label}|graph`), K = 4, S = 6, list = [], set = new Set(), deg = new Array(K * S).fill(0);
  const add = (u, v) => { list.push([u, v]); set.add(edgeKey(u, v)); deg[u]++; deg[v]++; };
  for (let k = 0; k < K; k++) {
    const nodes = shuffled(Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => k * S + i), rnd);
    for (let i = 1; i < S; i++) { const open = nodes.slice(0, i).filter((x) => deg[x] < 4); add(nodes[i], open[randInt(open.length, rnd)]); }
    let inMod = S - 1, guard = 0;
    while (inMod < 8 && ++guard < 10000) { const u = nodes[randInt(S, rnd)], v = nodes[randInt(S, rnd)]; if (u === v || set.has(edgeKey(u, v)) || deg[u] >= 4 || deg[v] >= 4) continue; add(u, v); inMod++; }
  }
  for (let k = 0; k < K; k++) {
    const a = Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => k * S + i).filter((x) => deg[x] < 4), b = Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => ((k + 1) % K) * S + i).filter((x) => deg[x] < 4);
    add(a[randInt(a.length, rnd)], b[randInt(b.length, rnd)]);
  }
  return plain(K * S, list);
}
// candidate B: 4 x 6 grid with 6 random edges removed, keeping it connected
function lattice(label) {
  const rnd = makeStream(`${label}|graph`), R = 4, C = 6, id = (r, c) => r * C + c, all = [];
  for (let r = 0; r < R; r++) for (let c = 0; c < C; c++) { if (c + 1 < C) all.push([id(r, c), id(r, c + 1)]); if (r + 1 < R) all.push([id(r, c), id(r + 1, c)]); }
  let list = [...all], removed = 0, guard = 0;
  while (removed < 6 && ++guard < 10000) { const i = randInt(list.length, rnd), cand = list.filter((_, j) => j !== i); if (connected(plain(R * C, cand))) { list = cand; removed++; } }
  return plain(R * C, list);
}

function describe(w, label) {
  const n = w.n, m = w.edges.length;
  let diam = 0; for (let s = 0; s < n; s++) diam = Math.max(diam, ...distancesTo(w, s));
  let cc = 0, ccN = 0; for (let u = 0; u < n; u++) { const nb = w.adj[u]; if (nb.length < 2) continue; let t = 0; for (let i = 0; i < nb.length; i++) for (let j = i + 1; j < nb.length; j++) if (w.adj[nb[i]].includes(nb[j])) t++; cc += t / (nb.length * (nb.length - 1) / 2); ccN++; }
  let bridges = 0; for (const [u, v] of w.edges) { const k = edgeKey(u, v); if (distancesTo(w, u, new Set([k]))[v] < 0) bridges++; }
  const plan = planE5(w, label, 'OFTEN');
  const dG = distancesTo(w, plan.goal), dS = distancesTo(w, plan.start), d = dG[plan.start];
  // number of shortest S-G routes (dynamic programming over BFS layers from S)
  const order = Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => i).sort((a, b) => dS[a] - dS[b]); const cnt = new Array(n).fill(0); cnt[plan.start] = 1;
  for (const u of order) for (const v of w.adj[u]) if (dS[v] === dS[u] + 1) cnt[v] += cnt[u];
  // detour excess of each scheduled event, given the permanent blocks before it (OFTEN, RARE schedules)
  const excess = [];
  let skipped = 0;
  for (const fam of ['RARE', 'OFTEN']) {
    const p = planE5(w, label, fam), perm = new Set();
    for (const e of p.events) {
      if (e.skipped) { skipped++; continue; }
      const before = distancesTo(w, p.goal, perm)[p.start], after = distancesTo(w, p.goal, new Set([...perm, edgeKey(...e.edge)]))[p.start];
      excess.push(after - before);
      if (!e.reverses) perm.add(edgeKey(...e.edge));
    }
  }
  return { n, m, cyclomatic: m - n + 1, diam, clustering: cc / ccN, bridgeShare: bridges / m, sg: d, routes: cnt[plan.goal], excess, skipped };
}
const q = (xs, p) => { const s = [...xs].sort((a, b) => a - b); return s[Math.min(s.length - 1, Math.floor(p * s.length))]; };
const mean = (xs) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / xs.length;
function summarise(name, items) {
  const ex = items.flatMap((x) => x.excess);
  const f = (k) => `${mean(items.map((x) => x[k])).toFixed(2)} [${q(items.map((x) => x[k]), 0.05).toFixed?.(2) ?? q(items.map((x) => x[k]), 0.05)}, ${q(items.map((x) => x[k]), 0.95).toFixed?.(2) ?? ''}]`;
  console.log(`== ${name} (${items.length} labels)`);
  console.log(`   n ${f('n')}  m ${f('m')}  cyclomatic ${f('cyclomatic')}  diameter ${f('diam')}  clustering ${f('clustering')}  bridge share ${f('bridgeShare')}`);
  console.log(`   S-G distance ${f('sg')}  shortest S-G routes ${f('routes')} (share with 1 route ${(items.filter((x) => x.routes === 1).length / items.length).toFixed(2)})`);
  console.log(`   detour excess per scheduled block: mean ${mean(ex).toFixed(2)}, share 0: ${(ex.filter((x) => x === 0).length / ex.length).toFixed(2)}, share >= 4: ${(ex.filter((x) => x >= 4).length / ex.length).toFixed(2)}, 95th pct ${q(ex, 0.95)}; skipped events ${items.reduce((s, x) => s + x.skipped, 0)}`);
}
const N = 60;
summarise('DEV generateWorld defaults (cf|dev|world|i)', Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => { const l = `cf|dev|world|${i}`; return describe(generateWorld(l), l); }));
const app = mfwWorld(path.join(ROOT, 'connections.json'), 'm13survey|app|graph');
summarise('APP graph (fixed topology; layouts vary)', Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => describe(app, `m13survey|app|${i}`)));
summarise('CANDIDATE modular ring (4 x 6)', Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => { const l = `m13survey|modular|${i}`; return describe(modular(l), l); }));
summarise('CANDIDATE perturbed lattice (4 x 6 minus 6)', Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => { const l = `m13survey|lattice|${i}`; return describe(lattice(l), l); }));
````

### experiments/m13_generalization/proposal_inputs/power_inputs.mjs

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/proposal_inputs/power_inputs.mjs` · SHA-256 (content) `96c31dc0195231a49ed383640e670c49223c3bfdc31436f92ef85a5c5e9b99b1` · 2893 bytes · 36 lines

````js
// M13 proposal input: per-label spread of the contrasts M13 would use, from the two existing records (CF-06 gate6 on
// generated worlds, M11 on the app graph). Reads stored rows only; runs nothing. Prints mean, SD, n, and the n needed
// for a two-sided 95% interval to exclude 0 with 80% power at several effect sizes.
import fs from 'node:fs';
const ROOT = process.argv[2];
const g = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`${ROOT}/experiments/cf06/results/gate6_result.json`));
const m = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`${ROOT}/experiments/m11_routes/results/m11_result.json`));
const stat = (d) => { const n = d.length, mu = d.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / n, sd = Math.sqrt(d.reduce((s, x) => s + (x - mu) ** 2, 0) / (n - 1)); return { n, mean: +mu.toFixed(2), sd: +sd.toFixed(2) }; };
const nFor = (sd, delta) => Math.ceil(((1.96 + 0.8416) * sd / delta) ** 2);
const crossings = (r) => r.events.reduce((s, e) => s + (!e.skipped && e.reverses ? e.crossed : 0), 0);
function contrasts(rows, armB, armM, armKey) {
  const by = (fam, arm) => new Map(rows.filter((r) => r.family === fam && r[armKey] === arm && !r.excluded).map((r) => [r.world, r]));
  const out = {};
  const pair = (fam, f, sign) => { const B = by(fam, armB), M = by(fam, armM); return [...B.keys()].filter((w) => M.has(w)).map((w) => sign * (f(B.get(w)) - f(M.get(w)))); };
  out.OFTEN_afterReversed = pair('OFTEN', (r) => r.windows.afterReversed, -1);
  out.OFTEN_total = pair('OFTEN', (r) => r.metric, -1);
  out.HALF_total = pair('HALF', (r) => r.metric, -1);
  out.RARE_cost = pair('RARE', (r) => r.metric, 1);
  out.RARE_afterPermanent_cost = pair('RARE', (r) => r.windows.afterPermanent, 1);
  out.RARE_futile_cost = pair('RARE', (r) => r.diagnostics.futileRetests, 1);
  out.OFTEN_crossings = pair('OFTEN', crossings, 1);
  // mix: per label, sum over the three families (CF-06 B1 unit)
  const fam = ['RARE', 'HALF', 'OFTEN'].map((f) => [by(f, armB), by(f, armM)]);
  const labels = [...fam[0][0].keys()].filter((w) => fam.every(([B, M]) => B.has(w) && M.has(w)));
  out.MIX_total = labels.map((w) => fam.reduce((s, [B, M]) => s + (M.get(w).metric - B.get(w).metric), 0));
  return out;
}
for (const [name, rows, key, b, mb] of [['CF-06 gate6 (generated worlds)', g.rows.e5, 'name', 'BELIEF', 'MBVI3'], ['M11 (app graph)', m.rows, 'arm', 'BELIEF', 'MBVI3']]) {
  console.log(`== ${name}`);
  for (const [k, d] of Object.entries(contrasts(rows, b, mb, key))) {
    const s = stat(d);
    console.log(`${k.padEnd(26)} n ${s.n}  mean ${String(s.mean).padStart(7)}  sd ${String(s.sd).padStart(7)}  n80% for delta = mean ${nFor(s.sd, Math.abs(s.mean))}, mean/2 ${nFor(s.sd, Math.abs(s.mean) / 2)}`);
  }
}
const w = g.resources.wallClockMsRunA;
console.log('gate6 seconds per E5 run (run A): BELIEF', (w.BELIEF / 1000 / 405).toFixed(2), 'MBVI3', (w.MBVI3 / 1000 / 405).toFixed(2), '; worlds', JSON.stringify(g.worlds).slice(0, 200));
````

## Appendix C. M13 implementation (generator, structure, measures, library, pins, driver)

### experiments/m13_generalization/worlds_modular.mjs

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/worlds_modular.mjs` · SHA-256 (content) `198a65ef63b4d36269506560581aa1e52d622f8b87f0f16c338febe81444eb25` · 5129 bytes · 85 lines

````js
// ==========================================================
// M13 — the held-out modular-ring family (ground truth; never imported by agents)
// ==========================================================
// A modular ring: MODULES modules of MODULE_SIZE nodes. Each module is a random spanning tree over its nodes plus
// random extra edges up to EDGES_PER_MODULE edges inside it. One bridge joins each module to the next, in a ring,
// between random nodes whose degree is below MAX_DEGREE. Every degree stays at or below MAX_DEGREE. Every stream is
// seeded by the label (`${label}|graph`), so the graph is a pure function of the label.
//
// The parameters were fixed in experiments/m13_generalization/PROPOSAL.md, from structure only, before any agent was
// run on this family: the same node count (24) and maximum degree (4) as the development family (generateWorld's
// defaults), so that neither size nor the number of exits per node differs.
//
// buildWorld is an exact copy of the testbed's private finish() in cognitive/testbed/worlds.mjs: the same hidden
// relabelling of nodes and exits, from the same stream (`${label}|perm`). It is copied rather than exported so that no
// testbed file changes. cognitive/tests/test_m13_generalization.mjs proves it gives worlds identical to generateWorld
// and mfwWorld for the same edges.
// ==========================================================
import { makeStream, randInt, shuffled } from '../../cognitive/testbed/rng.mjs';
import { edgeKey, isConnected } from '../../cognitive/testbed/worlds.mjs';

export const MODULAR = Object.freeze({ modules: 4, moduleSize: 6, edgesPerModule: 8, maxDegree: 4 });
export const BANK_LABEL = (i) => `m13|modular|${i}`;

/** The edge list (in generation order) of the modular ring for one label. */
export function modularEdges(label, P = MODULAR) {
  const rnd = makeStream(`${label}|graph`);
  const K = P.modules, S = P.moduleSize, D = P.maxDegree, n = K * S;
  const list = [], set = new Set(), deg = new Array(n).fill(0);
  const add = (u, v) => { list.push([u, v]); set.add(edgeKey(u, v)); deg[u]++; deg[v]++; };
  for (let k = 0; k < K; k++) {
    const nodes = shuffled(Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => k * S + i), rnd);
    for (let i = 1; i < S; i++) {
      const open = nodes.slice(0, i).filter((x) => deg[x] < D);
      if (!open.length) throw new Error(`${label}: module ${k} tree cannot respect the maximum degree`);
      add(nodes[i], open[randInt(open.length, rnd)]);
    }
    let inModule = S - 1, guard = 0;
    while (inModule < P.edgesPerModule && ++guard < 10000) {
      const u = nodes[randInt(S, rnd)], v = nodes[randInt(S, rnd)];
      if (u === v || set.has(edgeKey(u, v)) || deg[u] >= D || deg[v] >= D) continue;
      add(u, v); inModule++;
    }
    if (inModule < P.edgesPerModule) throw new Error(`${label}: module ${k} cannot place ${P.edgesPerModule} edges`);
  }
  for (let k = 0; k < K; k++) {
    const a = Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => k * S + i).filter((x) => deg[x] < D);
    const b = Array.from({ length: S }, (_, i) => ((k + 1) % K) * S + i).filter((x) => deg[x] < D);
    if (!a.length || !b.length) throw new Error(`${label}: no free node for the bridge after module ${k}`);
    add(a[randInt(a.length, rnd)], b[randInt(b.length, rnd)]);
  }
  return { n, edges: list };
}

/** Exact copy of worlds.mjs finish(label, n, edgeList, label): the world object with its hidden relabelling. */
export function buildWorld(label, n, edgeList) {
  const adj = Array.from({ length: n }, () => []);
  for (const [u, v] of edgeList) { adj[u].push(v); adj[v].push(u); }
  for (const a of adj) a.sort((x, y) => x - y);
  const rnd = makeStream(`${label}|perm`);
  const labels = shuffled(Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => i + 1), rnd);
  const exitOrder = adj.map((nb) => shuffled(nb, rnd));
  const nodeOfLabel = new Map(labels.map((l, i) => [l, i]));
  const edges = edgeList.map(([u, v]) => (u < v ? [u, v] : [v, u])).sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0] || a[1] - b[1]);
  const w = { id: label, n, edges, adj, labelOf: labels, exitOrder, nodeOfLabel };
  if (!isConnected(w)) throw new Error(`world ${label} is not connected`);
  return Object.freeze(w);
}

export const modularWorld = (label) => { const { n, edges } = modularEdges(label); return buildWorld(label, n, edges); };

/** The graph bank: every label's edge list, canonical (each edge [min, max], sorted), with the generator's parameters. */
export function makeBank(count) {
  const graphs = Array.from({ length: count }, (_, i) => {
    const label = BANK_LABEL(i), { n, edges } = modularEdges(label);
    return { label, n, edges: edges.map(([u, v]) => (u < v ? [u, v] : [v, u])).sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0] || a[1] - b[1]) };
  });
  return { family: 'modular ring', generator: { ...MODULAR }, count, graphs };
}

/** Checks that a bank is exactly what the generator gives for its labels. Throws on any difference. */
export function checkBank(bank) {
  const fresh = makeBank(bank.count);
  if (JSON.stringify(fresh) !== JSON.stringify(bank)) throw new Error('the graph bank differs from the generator');
  return true;
}
````

### experiments/m13_generalization/structure.mjs

File: `experiments/m13_generalization/structure.mjs` · SHA-256 (content) `5bae3e3aed34fe8d8d1a0d4505a30631526d2dc161a2609d2d6ced8f6f89a7ea` · 5977 bytes · 90 lines

````js
// ==========================================================
// M13 — structural descriptors, the novelty check and each event's detour excess (STRUCTURE ONLY)
// ==========================================================
// Everything here reads the true graph and task E5's schedule (planE5, which also reads only the graph). No agent is
// built and no environment is stepped. It is the survey code of PROPOSAL.md, kept here as the frozen definition.
//
// Novelty check (SPEC.md, G0), against the development family = generateWorld's defaults on the development labels
// cf|dev|world|0 ... cf|dev|world|164 (as many as the bank):
//   (a) the bank's 5th-percentile clustering exceeds the development family's 95th percentile;
//   (b) every bank graph has cyclomatic number 13 and every development graph 7.
// Percentiles use the index floor(p * n) of the sorted values.
// ==========================================================
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { generateWorld, mfwWorld, distancesTo, edgeKey } from '../../cognitive/testbed/worlds.mjs';
import { planE5 } from '../../cognitive/testbed/task_e5.mjs';
import { buildWorld } from './worlds_modular.mjs';

const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..');

export const DEV_REFERENCE = Object.freeze({ label: (i) => `cf|dev|world|${i}`, count: 165 });
export const quantile = (xs, p) => { const s = [...xs].sort((a, b) => a - b); return s[Math.min(s.length - 1, Math.floor(p * s.length))]; };
const mean = (xs) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / xs.length;

/** Detour excess of each scheduled event of one (world, family): shortest S-G distance with the block minus without. */
export function detourExcess(world, label, family) {
  const p = planE5(world, label, family), perm = new Set();
  return p.events.map((e) => {
    if (e.skipped) return null;
    const before = distancesTo(world, p.goal, perm)[p.start], after = distancesTo(world, p.goal, new Set([...perm, edgeKey(...e.edge)]))[p.start];
    if (!e.reverses) perm.add(edgeKey(...e.edge));
    return after - before;
  });
}

export function describe(world, label) {
  const n = world.n, m = world.edges.length;
  let diameter = 0; for (let s = 0; s < n; s++) diameter = Math.max(diameter, ...distancesTo(world, s));
  let cc = 0, ccN = 0;
  for (let u = 0; u < n; u++) {
    const nb = world.adj[u]; if (nb.length < 2) continue;
    let t = 0; for (let i = 0; i < nb.length; i++) for (let j = i + 1; j < nb.length; j++) if (world.adj[nb[i]].includes(nb[j])) t++;
    cc += t / (nb.length * (nb.length - 1) / 2); ccN++;
  }
  let bridges = 0; for (const [u, v] of world.edges) if (distancesTo(world, u, new Set([edgeKey(u, v)]))[v] < 0) bridges++;
  const plan = planE5(world, label, 'OFTEN');
  const dS = distancesTo(world, plan.start), dG = distancesTo(world, plan.goal);
  const order = Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => i).sort((a, b) => dS[a] - dS[b]);
  const routes = new Array(n).fill(0); routes[plan.start] = 1;
  for (const u of order) for (const v of world.adj[u]) if (dS[v] === dS[u] + 1) routes[v] += routes[u];
  const excess = ['RARE', 'HALF', 'OFTEN'].flatMap((f) => detourExcess(world, label, f));
  return {
    n, m, cyclomatic: m - n + 1, diameter, clustering: cc / ccN, bridgeShare: bridges / m, startGoal: dG[plan.start], shortestRoutes: routes[plan.goal],
    excess: excess.filter((x) => x !== null), skipped: excess.filter((x) => x === null).length,
  };
}

export function summarise(items) {
  const ex = items.flatMap((x) => x.excess);
  const q = (k) => { const xs = items.map((x) => x[k]); return { mean: mean(xs), p05: quantile(xs, 0.05), p95: quantile(xs, 0.95) }; };
  return {
    labels: items.length, n: q('n'), m: q('m'), cyclomatic: q('cyclomatic'), diameter: q('diameter'), clustering: q('clustering'), bridgeShare: q('bridgeShare'),
    startGoal: q('startGoal'), shortestRoutes: q('shortestRoutes'), singleRouteShare: items.filter((x) => x.shortestRoutes === 1).length / items.length,
    detourExcess: { mean: mean(ex), zeroShare: ex.filter((x) => x === 0).length / ex.length, fourPlusShare: ex.filter((x) => x >= 4).length / ex.length, p95: quantile(ex, 0.95) },
    skippedEvents: items.reduce((s, x) => s + x.skipped, 0), events: ex.length + items.reduce((s, x) => s + x.skipped, 0),
  };
}

/** The novelty check on a list of [label, world] for the bank. Returns the record and whether it holds. */
export function noveltyCheck(bankWorlds) {
  const bank = bankWorlds.map(([label, w]) => describe(w, label));
  const dev = Array.from({ length: DEV_REFERENCE.count }, (_, i) => { const l = DEV_REFERENCE.label(i); return describe(generateWorld(l), l); });
  const bankP05 = quantile(bank.map((x) => x.clustering), 0.05), devP95 = quantile(dev.map((x) => x.clustering), 0.95);
  const clusteringSeparated = bankP05 > devP95;
  const cyclomaticAsDeclared = bank.every((x) => x.cyclomatic === 13) && dev.every((x) => x.cyclomatic === 7);
  return {
    rule: 'bank 5th-percentile clustering > development 95th percentile; cyclomatic number 13 in every bank graph and 7 in every development graph',
    bankClusteringP05: bankP05, devClusteringP95: devP95, clusteringSeparated, cyclomaticAsDeclared,
    holds: clusteringSeparated && cyclomaticAsDeclared,
    bank: summarise(bank), development: summarise(dev),
  };
}

/** The structural record frozen with the specification: the novelty check, and the app graph's descriptors beside it. */
export function structureRecord(bank) {
  const worlds = bank.graphs.map((g) => [g.label, buildWorld(g.label, g.n, g.edges)]);
  const app = mfwWorld(path.join(ROOT, 'connections.json'), 'm13|structure|app');
  const appItems = Array.from({ length: bank.count }, (_, i) => describe(app, `m13|structure|app|${i}`));
  return { note: 'structure only: graphs and planE5 schedules; no agent was built or run', novelty: noveltyCheck(worlds), appGraph: summarise(appItems) };
}
````
